The first American case was reported on January 20, and then-President Donald Trump declared the U. S. outbreak a public health emergency on January 31. Restrictions were placed on flights

## ![](_page_24_Picture_9.jpeg)

The hospital ship USNS Comfort arrives in Manhattan on 30 March 2020.

arriving from China, [464][465] but the initial U. S. response to the pandemic was otherwise slow, in terms of preparing the healthcare system, stopping other travel, and testing. [466][467][468][g] Meanwhile, Trump downplayed the threat posed by the virus and claimed the outbreak was under control. [470]

The first known American deaths occurred in February. [471][h] On March 6, Trump signed the Coronavirus Preparedness and Response Supplemental Appropriations Act, which provided \$8.3 billion in emergency funding for federal agencies to respond to the outbreak. [472] On March 13, President Trump declared a national emergency. [473] In mid-March, the Trump administration started to purchase large quantities of medical equipment, [474] and in late March, it invoked the Defense Production Act to direct industries to produce medical equipment. [475] By April 17, the federal government approved disaster declarations for all states and territories. By mid-April, cases had been confirmed in all fifty U. S. states, and by November in all inhabited U. S. territories. A second rise in infections began in June 2020, following relaxed restrictions in several states. [476]

#### **South America**

The pandemic was confirmed to have reached South America on 26 February 2020 when Brazil confirmed a case in São Paulo. [478] By 3 April, all countries and territories in South America had recorded at least one case. [479]

On 13 May, it was reported that Latin America and the Caribbean had reported over 400,000 cases of infection with 23,091 deaths. On 22 May, citing especially the rapid increase of infections in Brazil, the WHO declared South America the epicentre of the pandemic. [480][481]

As of 20 September, South America has about 7.5 million confirmed cases and 238,000 deaths. Due to a dearth of testing and medical facilities, it is believed that the outbreak is far larger than the official numbers show. [482]

## ![](_page_25_Picture_4.jpeg)

Argentina was among the Latin American countries that earned the best grades for their response to the pandemic. [477]

#### **Brazil**

## ![](_page_25_Picture_7.jpeg)

Doctors check on a patient on a ventilator at In Cor in São Paulo.

## On 20 May it was reported that

Brazil had a record 1,179 deaths in a single day, for a total of almost 18,000 fatalities. With a total number of almost 272,000 cases, Brazil became the country with the third-highest number of cases, following Russia and the United States. [483] On 25 May, Brazil exceeded the number of reported cases in Russia when they reported that 11,687 new cases had been confirmed over the previous 24 hours, bringing the total number to over 374,800, with more than 23,400 deaths. President Jair Bolsonaro has created a great deal of controversy referring to the virus as a "little flu" and frequently speaking out against preventive measures such as lockdowns and quarantines. His attitude towards the outbreak has so closely matched that of President Trump he has been called the "Trump of the Tropics". [484] Bolsonaro later tested positive for the virus. [485]

In June 2020, the government of Brazil attempted to conceal the actual figures of the COVID-19 active cases and deaths, as it stopped publishing the total number of infections and deaths. On 5 June, Brazil's health ministry took down the official website reflecting the total numbers of infections and deaths. The website was live on 6 June, with only the number of infections of the previous 24 hours. The last official numbers reported about 615,000 infections and over 34,000 deaths. [486] On 15 June, it was reported that the worldwide cases had jumped from seven to eight million in one week, citing Latin America, specifically Brazil as one of the countries where cases are surging, in this case, towards 1 million cases. [487] Brazil briefly paused Phase III trials for the CoronavacCOVID-19 vaccine on 10 November after the suicide of a volunteer before resuming on 11 November. [488]

#### **Africa**

The pandemic was confirmed to have spread to Africa on 14 February 2020, with the first confirmed case announced in Egypt.[489][490] The first confirmed case in sub-Saharan Africa was announced in Nigeria at the end of February. [491] Within three months, the virus had spread throughout the continent, as Lesotho, the last African sovereign state to have remained free of the virus, reported a case on 13 May. [492][493] By 26 May, it appeared that most African countries were experiencing community transmission, although testing capacity was limited. [494] Most of the identified imported cases arrived from Europe and the United States rather than from China where the virus originated. [495] It is believed that there is widespread United States rather than from China where the virus originated. [495] It is believed that there is widespread United States rather than from China where the virus originated. [495] It is believed systems. [496]

#### Oceania

The pandemic was confirmed to have reached Oceania on 25 January 2020 with the first confirmed case reported in Melbourne, Victoria, Australia. [497] It has since spread elsewhere in the region, [498] although many small Pacific island nations have thus far avoided the outbreak by closing their international borders. Five Oceania sovereign states have yet to report a case: Kiribati, Nauru, Palau, Tonga, and Tuvalu. Australia and New Zealand have been praised for their handling of the pandemic compared to other western nations, with the latter wiping out all community transmission of the virus. The most recent country or territory to report its first confirmed case was the Federated States of Micronesia, on 8 January 2021.

## ![](_page_26_Picture_4.jpeg)

U. S. Air Force personnel unload a C-17 aircraft carrying approximately 1,800 kg (4,000 lb) of medical supplies in Niamey, Niger.

#### **Antarctica**

Due to its remoteness and sparse population, Antarctica was the last continent to have confirmed cases of COVID-19 and was one of the last regions of the world affected directly by the pandemic. [499][500][501] The first cases were reported in December 2020, almost a year after the first cases of COVID-19 were detected in China. At least 36 people are confirmed to have been infected. [502] Even before the first cases on the continent were reported, human activity in Antarctica was indirectly impacted.

# **International responses**

### **Travel restrictions**

As a result of the pandemic, many countries and regions imposed quarantines, entry bans, or other restrictions, either for citizens, recent travellers to affected areas, [503] or for all travellers. [504] Together with a decreased willingness to travel, this had a negative economic and social impact on the travel sector. Concerns have been raised over the effectiveness of travel restrictions to contain the spread of COVID-19. [505] A study in *Science* found that travel restrictions had only modestly affected the initial spread of COVID-19, unless combined with infection prevention and control measures to considerably reduce transmissions. [506] Researchers concluded that "travel restrictions are most useful in the early and late phase of an epidemic" and "restrictions of travel from Wuhan unfortunately came too late". [507]

The European Union rejected the idea of suspending the Schengen free travel zone and introducing border controls with Italy, [508][509] a decision which has been criticised by some European politicians. [510][511]

# **Evacuation of foreign citizens**

Owing to the effective lockdown of Wuhan and Hubei, several countries evacuated their citizens and diplomatic staff from the area, primarily through chartered flights of the home nation, with Chinese authorities providing clearance. Canada, the United States, Japan, India, [512] Sri Lanka, Australia, France, Argentina, Germany, and Thailand were among the first to plan the evacuation of their citizens. [513] Brazil and New Zealand also evacuated their own nationals and some other people. [514][515]

On 14 March, South Africa repatriated 112 South Africans who tested negative for the virus from Wuhan, while four who showed symptoms were left behind to mitigate risk.[\[516\]](#page-75-7) Pakistan said it would not evacuate citizens from China.[\[517\]](#page-75-8)

On 15 February, the U. S. announced it would evacuate Americans aboard the cruise ship *[Diamond Princess](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diamond_Princess_outbreak)*, [\[518\]](#page-75-9) and on 21 February, Canada evacuated 129 Canadian passengers from the ship.[\[519\]](#page-75-10) In early March, the Indian government began evacuating its citizens from Iran.[\[520\]](#page-76-0)[\[521\]](#page-76-1) On 20 March, the United States began to partially [withdraw its troops from Iraq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Withdrawal_of_U. S._troops_from_Iraq_(2020)) due to the pandemic.[\[522\]](#page-76-2)

## ![](_page_27_Picture_4.jpeg)

Ukraine evacuates Ukrainian and foreign citizens from [Wuhan,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wuhan) China.

# **United Nations response measures**

The [United Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations) response to the pandemic has been led by its [Secretary-General](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secretary-General_of_the_United_Nations) and can be divided into formal [resolutions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_resolution) at the [General Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly) and the [Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council) (UNSC), and operations via its specialised [agencies.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_System)

In June 2020, the Secretary-General launched its 'UN Comprehensive Response to COVID-19'.[\[523\]](#page-76-3) The [United Nations Conference on Trade and Development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Conference_on_Trade_and_Development) (UNSC) has been criticised for a slow coordinated response, especially regarding the UN's [global ceasefire,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_ceasefire) which aims to open up humanitarian access to the world's most vulnerable in conflict zones.[\[524\]](#page-76-4)

#### **WHO response measures**

The WHO is a leading organisation involved in the global coordination for mitigating the pandemic.

The WHO has spearheaded several initiatives like the [COVID-19 Solidarity Response Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_Solidarity_Response_Fund) to raise money for the pandemic response, the [UN COVID-19 Supply Chain Task Force,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_COVID-19_Supply_Chain_Task_Force) and the [solidarity trial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solidarity_trial) for investigating potential treatment options for the disease. The WHO's COVAX vaccine-sharing program aims to distribute 2 billion doses of COVID-19 vaccine for free or at a reduced cost by the end of 2021, but has struggled to raise funds to subsidize the costs.[\[525\]](#page-76-5)

The WHO's handling of the initial outbreak of the pandemic has required a "diplomatic balancing act" between member states, in particular between the United States and China.[\[526\]](#page-76-6) On August 27, the WHO announced the setting up of an independent expert Review Committee to examine aspects of the international treaty that governs preparedness and response to health emergencies. A WHO-led [international mission arrived in China in January 2021 to investigate the origins of the COVID-19](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Investigations_into_the_origin_of_COVID-19) pandemic.

# **Protests against governmental measures**

In several countries, protests have risen against governmental restrictive responses to the COVID-19 pandemic, such as lockdowns.

# **Impact**

# **Economics**

The outbreak is a major destabilising threat to the global [economy. Agathe Demarais of the Economist Intelligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economist_Intelligence_Unit) Unit has forecast that markets will remain volatile until a clearer image emerges on potential outcomes. One [estimate from an expert at Washington University in St.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_University_in_St._Louis) Louis gave a \$300+ billion impact on the world's supply chain that could last up to two years.[\[527\]](#page-76-7) Global stock markets fell on 24 February due to a significant rise in the number of COVID-19 cases outside China.[\[528\]](#page-76-8)[\[529\]](#page-76-9) On 27 February, due to mounting worries about the coronavirus outbreak, U. S. stock indexes posted their sharpest falls since 2008, with the [Dow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dow_Jones_Industrial_Average) falling 1,191 points (the largest one-day drop since the [financial crisis of 2007–08](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_crisis_of_2007%E2%80%9308)) [\[530\]](#page-76-10) and all three major indexes ending the week down more than 10 percent.[\[531\]](#page-76-11) On 28 February, Scope Ratings

## ![](_page_28_Figure_4.jpeg)

A stock [index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stock_index) chart shows the 2020 stock [market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_stock_market_crash) crash.

GmbH affirmed China's sovereign credit rating but maintained a Negative Outlook.[\[532\]](#page-76-12) Stocks plunged again due to coronavirus fears, the largest fall being on 16 March.[\[533\]](#page-76-13)

[Lloyd's of London](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd%27s_of_London) has estimated that the global insurance industry will absorb losses of US\$204 billion, exceeding the losses from the [2017 Atlantic hurricane season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Atlantic_hurricane_season) and [11 September attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/September_11_attacks), suggesting the COVID-19 pandemic will likely go down in history as the costliest disaster ever in human history.[\[534\]](#page-76-14)

## ![](_page_28_Picture_8.jpeg)

A highway sign in [Toronto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toronto) discouraging non-essential travel during the pandemic lockdown in March 2020

Tourism is one of the worst affected sectors due to travel bans, closing of public places including travel attractions, and advice of governments against travel. Numerous airlines have cancelled flights due to lower demand, and British regional airline [Flybe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flybe) collapsed.[\[535\]](#page-76-15) The cruise line industry was hard hit,[\[536\]](#page-76-16) and several train stations and ferry ports have also been closed.[\[537\]](#page-77-0) International mail between some countries stopped or was delayed due to reduced transportation between them or suspension of domestic service.[\[538\]](#page-77-1)

The retail sector has been impacted globally, with reductions in store hours or temporary closures.[\[539\]](#page-77-2) Visits to retailers in Europe and Latin America declined by 40 percent. North America and Middle East retailers saw a 50–60 percent drop.[\[540\]](#page-77-3) This also resulted in a

33–43 percent drop in foot traffic to shopping centres in March compared to February. Shopping mall operators around the world imposed additional measures, such as increased sanitation, installation of thermal scanners to check the temperature of shoppers, and cancellation of events.[\[541\]](#page-77-4)

[Hundreds of millions of jobs could be lost globally.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unemployment_in_the_United_States)[\[542\]](#page-77-5)[\[543\]](#page-77-6) More than 40 million Americans lost their jobs and filed [unemployment insurance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unemployment_insurance) claims.[\[544\]](#page-77-7) The economic impact and mass unemployment caused by the pandemic has raised fears of a mass [eviction crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eviction_in_the_United_States), [\[545\]](#page-77-8)[\[546\]](#page-77-9) with an analysis by the Aspen Institute [indicating between 30 and 40 million Americans are at risk for eviction by the end of](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aspen_Institute) 2020.[\[547\]](#page-77-10)[\[548\]](#page-77-11) According to a report by [Yelp,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yelp) about 60% of U. S. businesses that have closed since the start of the pandemic will stay shut permanently.[\[549\]](#page-77-12)

[According to a United Nations Economic Commission for Latin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Economic_Commission_for_Latin_America_and_the_Caribbean) America estimate, the pandemic-induced recession could leave 14– 22 million more people in [extreme poverty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extreme_poverty) in Latin America than would have been in that situation without the pandemic.[\[550\]](#page-77-13) According to the [World Bank,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Bank) up to 100 million more people globally could fall into extreme poverty due to the shutdowns.[\[551\]](#page-77-14)[\[552\]](#page-77-15) The [International Labour Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Labour_Organization) (ILO) informed that the income generated in the first nine months of 2020 from work across the world dropped by 10.7 per cent, or \$3.5 trillion, amidst the coronavirus outbreak.[\[553\]](#page-77-16)

#### **Supply shortages**

[The outbreak has been blamed for several instances of supply](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shortage) shortages, stemming from globally increased usage of equipment to fight outbreaks, panic buying (which in several places led to shelves being cleared of grocery essentials such as food, toilet paper, and bottled water), and disruption to factory and logistic operations.[\[554\]](#page-77-17) The spread of panic buying has been found to stem from perceived threat, perceived scarcity, fear of the unknown, coping behaviour and social psychological factors (e.g. [social influence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_influence) and trust).[\[555\]](#page-78-0) The technology industry, in particular, has warned of delays to shipments of electronic goods.[\[556\]](#page-78-1) According to the WHO directorgeneral [Tedros Adhanom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tedros_Adhanom), demand for personal protection equipment has risen a hundredfold, leading to prices up to twenty times the normal price and also delays in the supply of medical items of four to six months.[\[557\]](#page-78-2)[\[558\]](#page-78-3) It has also caused a shortage of personal protective equipment worldwide, with the WHO warning that this will endanger health workers.[\[559\]](#page-78-4)

## ![](_page_29_Picture_5.jpeg)

"Those who can, put something in; those who can't, help yourself." [Bologna,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bologna) April 2020.

## ![](_page_29_Picture_7.jpeg)

Coronavirus fears have led to panic buying of essentials across the world, including toilet [paper,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toilet_paper) dried and instant [noodles,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Instant_noodles) bread, rice, vegetables, disinfectant, and [rubbing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rubbing_alcohol) alcohol.

The impact of the coronavirus outbreak was worldwide. The virus created a shortage of [precursors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Precursor_(chemistry)) (raw material) used in the manufacturing of [fentanyl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fentanyl) and [methamphetamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Methamphetamine). The [Yuancheng Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuancheng_Group), headquartered in Wuhan, is one of the leading suppliers.[\[560\]](#page-78-5) Price increases and shortages in these illegal drugs have been noticed on the streets of the UK.[\[561\]](#page-78-6) U. S. law enforcement also told the *New York Post* [Mexican drug cartels were having difficulty in obtaining precursors.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Post)[\[562\]](#page-78-7)

The pandemic has disrupted global [food supplies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_security) and threatens to trigger a new [food crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_crisis). [\[563\]](#page-78-8)[\[564\]](#page-78-9) David Beasley, head of the [World Food Programme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Food_Programme) (WFP), said "we could be facing multiple [famines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Famine) of biblical proportions within a short few months."[\[565\]](#page-78-10) Senior officials at the United Nations estimated in April 2020 that an additional 130 million people could [starve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Starvation), for a total of 265 million by the end of 2020.[\[565\]](#page-78-10)[\[566\]](#page-78-11)[\[567\]](#page-78-12)

#### **Oil and other energy markets**

In early February 2020, [Organization of the Petroleum Exporting Countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OPEC) (OPEC) "scrambled" after a steep decline in [oil prices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Price_of_oil) due to lower demand from China.[\[568\]](#page-78-13) On Monday, 20 April, the price of West Texas Intermediate (WTI) went negative and fell to a record low (minus \$37.63 a barrel) due to traders' offloading holdings so as not to take delivery and incur storage costs.[\[569\]](#page-78-14) June prices were down but in the positive range, with a barrel of West Texas trading above \$20.[\[569\]](#page-78-14)

#### Culture

The performing arts and cultural heritage sectors have been profoundly affected by the pandemic, impacting organisations' operations as well as individuals—both employed and independent—globally. Arts and culture sector organisations attempted to uphold their (often publicly funded) mission to provide access to cultural heritage to the community, maintain the safety of their employees and the public, and support artists where possible. By March 2020, across the world and to varying degrees, museums, libraries, performance venues, and other cultural institutions had been indefinitely closed with their exhibitions, events and performances cancelled or postponed. [570] In response there were intensive efforts to provide alternative services through digital platforms. [571][572][573]

Holy Week observances in Rome, which occur during the last week of the Christian penitential season of Lent, were cancelled. [572] Many dioceses have recommended older Christians stay home rather than attend Mass on Sundays; services have been made available via radio, online live streaming and television, though some congregations made have provisions worship. [574][575][572] With the Roman Catholic Diocese of Rome closing its churches and chapels and St. Peter's Square emptied of Christian pilgrims, [572] other religious bodies also cancelled inperson services and limited public gatherings in churches, mosques, synagogues, temples and gurdwaras. [572] Iran's Health Ministry announced the cancellation of Friday prayers in areas affected by the outbreak and shrines were later closed, [323][330] while Saudi Arabia banned the entry of foreign pilgrims as well as its residents to holy sites in Mecca and Medina. [576][577] The 2020 Hajj was limited to around 1,000 selected pilgrims, in contrast to the usual number of over 2 million. [578]

## ![](_page_30_Picture_5.jpeg)

An American Catholic military chaplain prepares for a livestreamed Mass in an empty chapel at Offutt Air Force Base in March 2020.

The pandemic has caused the most significant disruption to the worldwide sporting calendar since the Second World War. Most major sporting events have been cancelled or postponed, including the 2019–20 UEFA Champions League, [579] 2019–20 Premier League, [580] UEFA Euro 2020, 2019–20 NBA season, [581] and 2019–20 NHL season. [582] The outbreak disrupted plans for the 2020 Summer Olympics in Tokyo, Japan, which were originally scheduled to start at 24 July 2020, and were postponed by the International Olympic Committee to 23 July 2021. [583][584][585]

The entertainment industry has also been affected, with many music groups suspending or cancelling concert tours. [586][587] The Eurovision Song Contest, which was due to be held in Rotterdam, the Netherlands in May, was cancelled; however, the Netherlands was retained as host for 2021. [588][589] Many large theatres such as those on Broadway also suspended all performances. [590] Some artists have explored ways to continue to produce and share work over the internet as an alternative to traditional live performance, such as live streaming concerts [591] or creating web-based "festivals" for artists to perform, distribute, and publicise their work. [592]

The large number of people working or learning from home via videoconferencing software led to several new terms and trends, including "zoom fatigue", [593] a decline in demand for formal clothing, and increased fashion focus on masks and clothes for the upper body (the lower body generally not being visible on a videoconference). [594] The term "doomscrolling" became more widely used. Online, numerous COVID-19-themed Internet memes have spread as many turn to humour and distraction amid the uncertainty.[\[595\]](#page-80-8) A popular Twitter account called "Room Rater" jokingly commented on and assigned numerical scores to the backgrounds of journalists and others appearing in mass media from home.[\[596\]](#page-80-9)

# **Politics**

The pandemic has affected the political systems of multiple countries, causing suspensions of legislative activities,[\[597\]](#page-80-10) isolations or deaths of multiple politicians,[\[598\]](#page-80-11) and rescheduling of elections due to fears of spreading the virus.[\[599\]](#page-80-12) Starting in late May, [large-scale protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Floyd_protests) against police brutality in at least [200 U. S. cities and l](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Floyd_protests#Impact_on_COVID-19_transmission)[ater worldwide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_George_Floyd_protests_outside_the_United_States) in response to the killing of [George Floyd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killing_of_George_Floyd) raised concerns of a resurgence of the virus. [\[600\]](#page-80-13)

Although they have broad support among epidemiologists, social distancing measures have been politically controversial in many countries. Intellectual opposition to social distancing has come primarily from writers of other fields, although there are a few heterodox epidemiologists.[\[601\]](#page-80-14)

On 23 March 2020, United Nations Secretary-General [António Manuel de Oliveira Guterres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ant%C3%B3nio_Guterres) issued an appeal for a global ceasefire in response to the pandemic;[\[602\]](#page-80-15)[\[603\]](#page-80-16) 172 UN Member States and Observers signed a non-binding statement in support of the appeal in June,[\[604\]](#page-80-17) and the [UN Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Security_Council) passed a [resolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_2532) supporting it in July.[\[605\]](#page-81-0)[\[606\]](#page-81-1)

#### **China**

The government of China has been criticised by the [United States,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_government_of_the_United_States) [\[607\]](#page-81-2) the UK Minister for the Cabinet Office [Michael Gove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Gove), [\[608\]](#page-81-3) and others[\[609\]](#page-81-4) for its handling of the pandemic. A number of provincial-level administrators of the [Communist Party of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_Party_of_China) were dismissed over their handling of the quarantine measures in China, a sign of discontent with their response to the outbreak. Some commentators believed this move was intended to protect CCP [general secretary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Secretary_of_the_Communist_Party) [Xi Jinping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xi_Jinping) from the controversy.[\[610\]](#page-81-5) The [U. S. intelligence community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Intelligence_Community) claims China intentionally under-reported its number of coronavirus cases.[\[611\]](#page-81-6) The Chinese government maintains it has acted swiftly and transparently.[\[612\]](#page-81-7)[\[613\]](#page-81-8)

### **Italy**

In early March, the Italian government criticised the EU's lack of solidarity with coronavirus-affected Italy[\[614\]](#page-81-9)[\[615\]](#page-81-10)—Maurizio Massari, Italy's ambassador to the EU, said "only China responded bilaterally", not the EU.[\[616\]](#page-81-11) On 22 March, after a phone call with Italian Prime Minister Giuseppe Conte, Russian president [Vladimir Putin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimir_Putin) had the [Russian army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Armed_Forces) send military medics, disinfection vehicles, and other medical equipment to Italy.[\[617\]](#page-81-12) [President of Lombardy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_Lombardy) [Attilio Fontana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attilio_Fontana) and Italian Foreign Minister [Luigi Di Maio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luigi_Di_Maio) expressed their gratitude for the aid.[\[618\]](#page-81-13) Russia also sent a cargo plane with medical aid to the United States.[\[619\]](#page-81-14) Kremlin spokesman [Dmitry Peskov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dmitry_Peskov) said "when offering assistance to U. S. colleagues, [Putin] assumes that when U. S. manufacturers of medical equipment and materials gain momentum, they will also be able to reciprocate if necessary."[\[620\]](#page-81-15) In early April, [Norway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norway) and EU states like [Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romania) and [Austria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austria) started to offer help by sending medical personnel and disinfectant,[\[621\]](#page-81-16) and [Ursula von der Leyen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ursula_von_der_Leyen) offered an official apology to the country.[\[622\]](#page-81-17)

#### **United States**

The outbreak prompted calls for the United States to adopt social policies common in other wealthy countries, including universal health care, universal child care, paid sick leave, and higher levels of funding for public health. [624][625][626] Political analysts believe it may have contributed to Donald Trump's loss in the 2020 presidential election. [627][628] Beginning in mid-April 2020, there were protests in several U. S. states against government-imposed business closures and restricted personal movement and association. [629] Simultaneously, protests ensued by essential workers in the form of a general strike. [630] In early October 2020, Donald Trump, his family members, and many other government officials were diagnosed with COVID-19, further disrupting the country's politics. [631]

## ![](_page_32_Picture_3.jpeg)

Several hundred anti-lockdown protesters rallied at the Ohio Statehouse 20 April. [623]

#### Other countries

The planned NATO "Defender 2020" military exercise in Germany, Poland, and the Baltic states, the largest NATO war exercise since the end of the Cold War, was held on a reduced scale. [632][633] The Campaign for Nuclear Disarmament's general secretary Kate Hudson criticised the exercise, saying "it jeopardises the lives not only of the troops from the U. S. and the many European countries participating but the inhabitants of the countries in which they are operating." [634]

The Iranian government has been heavily affected by the virus, with about two dozen parliament members and fifteen current or former political figures infected. [333][635] Iran's President Hassan Rouhani wrote a public letter to world leaders asking for help on 14 March 2020, saying they were struggling to fight the outbreak due to a lack of access to international markets from the United States sanctions against Iran. [636] Saudi Arabia, which launched a military intervention in Yemen in March 2015, declared a ceasefire. [637]

Diplomatic relations between Japan and South Korea worsened due to the pandemic. South Korea criticised Japan's "ambiguous and passive quarantine efforts" after Japan announced anyone coming from South Korea would be placed in quarantine for two weeks at government-designated sites. South Korean society was initially polarised on President Moon Jae-in's response to the crisis; many Koreans signed petitions either calling for Moon's impeachment or praising his response.

Some countries have passed emergency legislation in response to the pandemic. Some commentators have expressed concern that it could allow governments to strengthen their grip on power. [640][641] In the Philippines, lawmakers granted president Rodrigo Duterte temporary emergency powers during the pandemic. In Hungary, the parliament voted to allow the prime minister, Viktor Orbán, to rule by decree indefinitely, suspend parliament as well as elections, and punish those deemed to have spread false information about the virus and the government's handling of the crisis. [643] In some countries, including Egypt, [644] Turkey, [645] and Thailand, [642] opposition activists and government critics have been arrested for allegedly spreading fake news about the COVID-19 pandemic. [646]

# Agriculture and food systems

The COVID-19 pandemic has disrupted agricultural and food systems worldwide. [647] COVID-19 hit at a time when hunger or undernourishment was once again on the rise in the world, with an estimated 690 million people already going hungry in 2019. [648] Based on the latest UN estimates, the economic recession triggered by the pandemic may lead to another 83 million people, and possibly as many as 132 million, going hungry in 2020. [648][649][650][651] This is mainly due to a lack of access to food – linked to falling incomes, lost remittances and, in some cases, a rise in food prices. In countries that already suffer from high levels of acute food insecurity, it is no longer an issue of access to food alone, but increasingly also one of food production. [647][648]

The pandemic, alongside lockdowns and travel restrictions, has prevented movement of aid and greatly impacted food production. As a result, several famines are forecast, which the UN called a crisis "of biblical proportions," [652] or "hunger pandemic." It is estimated that without intervention 30 million people may die of hunger, with Oxfam reporting that "12,000 people per day could die from COVID-19 linked hunger" by the end of $2020. \frac{[654][652][655]}{[652][655]}$ This pandemic, in conjunction with the 2019-20 locust infestations and several ongoing armed conflicts, is predicted to form the worst series of famines since the Great Chinese Famine, affecting between 10 and 20 percent of the global population in some way. 656 55 countries are reported to be at risk, with three dozen succumbing to crisis-level famines or above in the worst-case scenario. 657 265 million people are forecast to be in famine conditions, an increase of 125 million due to the coronavirus pandemic.

#### **Education**

The pandemic has severely impacted educational systems globally. Most governments have temporarily closed educational institutions, with many switching to online education. As of 30 September 2020, approximately 1.077 billion learners are currently affected due to school closures in response to the pandemic. According to UNICEF monitoring, 53 countries are currently implementing nationwide closures and 27 are implementing local closures, impacting about 61.6 percent of the world's student population. 72 countries' schools are currently open. [658]

School closures impact not only students, teachers, and families but have far-reaching economic and societal consequences. School closures in response to the pandemic have shed light on various social and economic issues, including student debt, digital learning, food insecurity, and homelessness, as well as access to childcare, health care, housing, internet, and disability services. The impact has been more severe for disadvantaged children and their families, causing interrupted learning, compromised nutrition, childcare problems, and consequent economic cost to families who cannot work.

#### Other health issues

The pandemic has had many impacts on global health beyond those caused by the COVID-19 disease itself. It has led to a reduction in hospital visits for other reasons. There have been 38 per cent fewer hospital visits for heart attack symptoms in the United States and 40 per cent fewer in Spain. The head of cardiology at the University of Arizona said, "My worry is some of these people are dying at home because they're too scared to go to the hospital. In the is also concern that people with strokes and appendicitis are not seeking timely treatment. Shortages of medical supplies have impacted people with various conditions. Shortages of medical supplies have impacted people with various conditions.

In several countries there has been a marked reduction of spread of sexually transmitted infections, including HIV/AIDS, attributable to COVID-19 quarantines, social distancing measures, and recommendations to not engage in casual sex. [662][663] Similarly, in some places, rates of transmission of influenza and other respiratory viruses significantly decreased during the pandemic. [664][665][666]

The pandemic has also negatively impacted mental health globally, including increased loneliness resulting from social distancing and depression and domestic violence from lockdowns. 668 As of

June 2020, 40% of U. S. adults were experiencing adverse mental health symptoms, with 11% having seriously considered trying to kill themselves in the past month.[\[669\]](#page-84-11)

# **Environment and climate**

The worldwide disruption caused by the pandemic has resulted in numerous effects on the [environment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_environment) and the [climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate). The global reduction in modern [human activity such as the considerable decline in](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_impact_of_the_COVID-19_pandemic#Transportation) planned travel[\[671\]](#page-85-0) was coined [anthropause](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthropause)[\[672\]](#page-85-1) and [has caused a large drop in a](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_pollution)[ir pollutio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_pollution)[n](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_pollution) and water pollution in many regions.[\[673\]](#page-85-2) In China, [lockdowns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_lockdown_in_Hubei) and other measures resulted in a 25 percent reduction in [carbon emissions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greenhouse_gas)[\[674\]](#page-85-3) and 50 percent reduction in nitrogen oxides emissions,[\[675\]](#page-85-4) which one [Earth systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Earth_system_science) scientist estimated may have saved at least 77,000 lives over two months.[\[676\]](#page-85-5)[\[677\]](#page-85-6) Other positive effects on the environment include [governance-](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governance)system-controlled investments towards a [sustainable energy transition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_transition) and other goals related to environmental protection such as the [European Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Union)'s seven-year €1 trillion budget [proposal and €750 billion recovery plan "Next](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Next_Generation_EU) Generation EU" which seeks to reserve 25% of EU spending for climate-friendly expenditure.[\[678\]](#page-85-7)[\[679\]](#page-85-8)[\[680\]](#page-85-9)

## ![](_page_34_Figure_5.jpeg)

Images from the NASA Earth [Observatory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NASA_Earth_Observatory) show a stark drop in pollution in [Wuhan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wuhan), when comparing [NO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nitrogen_dioxide)2 levels in early 2019 (top) and early 2020 (bottom). [\[670\]](#page-84-10)

[However, the outbreak has also provided cover for illegal activities such as deforestation of the Amazon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deforestation_of_the_Amazon_rainforest) rainforest[\[681\]](#page-85-10)[\[682\]](#page-85-11) and [poaching](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poaching) in Africa,[\[683\]](#page-86-0)[\[684\]](#page-86-1) hindered [environmental diplomacy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_policy) efforts,[\[685\]](#page-86-2) and created [economic fallout](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coronavirus_recession) that some predict will slow investment in [green energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_energy) technologies.[\[686\]](#page-86-3)

# **Xenophobia and racism**

Since the start of the outbreak, heightened prejudice, xenophobia, and racism have been documented around the world [toward people of Chinese and East Asian descent.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinophobe) [\[687\]](#page-86-4)[\[688\]](#page-86-5)[\[689\]](#page-86-6) Reports from February (when most cases were confined to China) documented racist sentiments expressed in groups worldwide about Chinese people 'deserving' the virus.[\[690\]](#page-86-7)[\[691\]](#page-86-8)[\[692\]](#page-86-9) Chinese people and other Asians in the United Kingdom and United States have reported increasing levels of racist abuse and assaults.[\[693\]](#page-86-10)[\[694\]](#page-86-11)[\[695\]](#page-86-12) U. S. President Donald Trump has been criticised for referring to the coronavirus as the "Chinese Virus" and "Kung Flu", which has been widely condemned as racist and xenophobic.[\[696\]](#page-86-13)[\[697\]](#page-87-0)[\[698\]](#page-87-1) On 14 March, an Asian family, including a two-year-old girl, was attacked at knifepoint in [Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas) in what the [FBI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FBI) has called a COVID-19 related hate crime.[\[699\]](#page-87-2)

Following the progression of the outbreak to new hotspot countries, people from Italy (the first country in Europe to experience a serious outbreak of COVID-19) were also subjected to suspicion and xenophobia,[\[700\]](#page-87-3)[\[701\]](#page-87-4) as were people from hotspots in other countries. Discrimination against Muslims in India [escalated after public health authorities identified an Islamic missionary \(Tablighi Jamaat\)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_India) group's gathering in New Delhi in early March 2020 as a source of spread. [\[702\]](#page-87-5) Paris has seen riots break out over police [treatment of ethnic minorities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_situation_in_the_French_suburbs) during the coronavirus lockdown.[\[703\]](#page-87-6) Racism and xenophobia towards [southern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Asian_ethnic_groups) [and south east Asians increased in the Arab states of the Persian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_states_of_the_Persian_Gulf) Gulf. [\[704\]](#page-87-7)[\[705\]](#page-87-8)[\[706\]](#page-87-9) South Korea's [LGBTQ community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_South_Korea) was blamed by some for the spread of COVID-19 in Seoul.[\[707\]](#page-87-10)[\[708\]](#page-87-11) In China, some [people of African descent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Africans_in_Guangzhou#COVID-19_pandemic) were evicted from their homes and told to leave China within 24 hours, due to disinformation that they and other foreigners were spreading the virus.[\[709\]](#page-87-12) This racism and xenophobia was criticised by foreign governments, diplomatic corps, and the Chinese ambassador to Zimbabwe.[\[710\]](#page-87-13)

# **Information dissemination**

Ongoing COVID-19 research is indexed and searchable in the [NIH](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NIH) COVID-19 Portfolio.[\[711\]](#page-87-14) Some newspaper agencies removed their online [paywalls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paywall) for some or all of their coronavirus-related articles and posts,[\[712\]](#page-87-15) while scientific publishers made scientific papers related to the outbreak available with [open access.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_access) [\[713\]](#page-87-16)[\[714\]](#page-88-1) Some scientists chose to share their results quickly on [preprint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Preprint) servers such as [bio Rxiv.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bio Rxiv) [\[715\]](#page-88-2)

Maps have played a key role in disseminating information concerning the spatial distribution of the disease, especially with the development of dashboards to present data in near real-time. Methods of data visualisation have drawn some criticism, however, in the over-simplification of geographical patterns indicated by choropleth maps that adopt national, rather than local, map scales.[\[716\]](#page-88-3)

# **Misinformation**

The pandemic has resulted in [misinformation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Misinformation) and [conspiracy theories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conspiracy_theory) about the scale of the pandemic and the [origin,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Investigations_into_the_origin_of_COVID-19) prevention, diagnosis, and treatment of [the disease](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coronavirus_disease_2019). False information, including intentional [disinformation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disinformation), has been [spread through social media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impact_of_the_COVID-19_pandemic_on_social_media#Misinformation), text messaging,[\[717\]](#page-88-4) and [mass media.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_coverage_of_the_COVID-19_pandemic) Journalists have been arrested for allegedly spreading [fake news](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fake_news) about the pandemic. It has also been propagated by celebrities, politicians, and other prominent public figures. A [Cornell University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cornell_University) study found that US President [Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump) was "likely the largest driver" of the COVID-19 misinformation [infodemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infodemic) in English-language media.[\[718\]](#page-88-5)

Commercial [scams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confidence_trick) have claimed to offer [at-home tests, supposed preventives, and "miracle" cures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_unproven_methods_against_COVID-19). [\[719\]](#page-88-6) Several religious groups have claimed their faith will protect them from the virus.[\[720\]](#page-88-7) Some people have claimed the virus is a [bioweapon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bioweapon) accidentally or purposefully leaked from a laboratory, a population [control scheme, the result of a spy operation, or the side effect of 5G upgrades to cellular networks.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_population_planning)[\[721\]](#page-88-8)

The WHO has declared an "infodemic" of incorrect information about the virus, which poses risks to global health.[\[722\]](#page-88-9)

# **Notes**

- a. In summary, this article is about the coronavirus *pandemic*, which is caused by the *disease* COVID‑19, which in turn is caused by the *virus* SARS‑CoV‑2.
- b. Known as "close contact" which is variously defined, including within ~1.8 metres (six feet) by the US [Centers for Disease Control and Prevention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centers_for_Disease_Control_and_Prevention) (CDC), and being face to face for a cumulative total of 15 minutes,[\[6\]](#page-36-15) or either 15 minutes of face to face proximity or sharing an enclosed space for a prolonged period such as two hours by the Australian Health Department.[\[7\]](#page-37-10)[\[8\]](#page-37-11)
- c. This CDC best estimate (current as of 10 September) was derived from a paper in PLOS Medicine that estimated a range of IFR across several areas in Europe, from 0.5% (95% CrI 0.4%–0.6%) in Switzerland to 1.4% (95% CrI 1.1%–1.6%) in Lombardy, Italy (based on data through mid-to-late April).[\[62\]](#page-41-7) Regarding the differences across the regions, the European researchers noted "the importance of local factors ... including demographic characteristics" and "the lower degree of preparedness and health service capacity in northern Italy, which in Europe was affected first by the SARS-CoV-2 epidemic."[\[62\]](#page-41-7)
- d. The overall value was corrected from 0.27% to account for "the fact that only one or two types of antibodies (among IgG, IgM, IgA) might have been used" in testing.
- e. [One set of national statistics for England \(ONS \(https://www.ons.gov.uk/peoplepopulationandcommu](https://www.ons.gov.uk/peoplepopulationandcommunity/healthandsocialcare/conditionsanddiseases/datasets/coronaviruscovid19infectionsurveydata) nity/healthandsocialcare/conditionsanddiseases/datasets/coronaviruscovid19infectionsurveydata)) [shows a decrease in IFR from 2.63% to 0.49%; another \(MRC \(https://www.mrc-bsu.cam.ac.uk/now](https://www.mrc-bsu.cam.ac.uk/now-casting)casting)) from 0.69% to 0.30%, both over eight weeks ending 4 August.
- f. Some refer to 'fatality rate'; however 'fatality ratio' is more accurate as this is not per unit time.[\[71\]](#page-42-1)
- g. A lack of mass testing obscured the extent of the outbreak.[\[469\]](#page-72-14)
- h. The earliest deaths were not known to be caused by COVID-19 until April.
